Consolidated CI Workflows\n**Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):\n\n- **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers\n- **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed\n- **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management\n\n### 2. Multiple Installation Methods\nUsers can now install packages via:\n\n**One-line installer:**\n```bash\ncurl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas\n```\n\n**Direct wheel download:**\n```bash\ncurl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl\npip install /tmp/pandas.whl\n```\n\n**CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)\n\n### 3. Immediate Package Access\nAdded pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.\n\n## Key Benefits\n- **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files\n- **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files\n- **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods\n- **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested\n- **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes\n\nThe solution directly addresses the \"bloated\" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.\n\nFixes #11.\n\n\u003c!-- START COPILOT CODING AGENT TIPS --\u003e\n---\n\n💡 You can make Copilot smarter by setting up custom instructions, customizing its development environment and configuring Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console\n2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section \n3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)\n4. Save the policy file\n\n## OAuth Client Configuration\n\nThe OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:\n\n1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)\n2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above\n3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)\n\nThe Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub
